</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type` | one of `database`, `external`, `bft-sequencer` | `reference` † | Choose one type — see [SequencerConfig](#sequencerconfig). |
| **sequencer** `type = database` |  |  |  |
|     `testing-interceptor` | string (TestingInterceptor) | _unset_ |  |
|     **high-availability** |  |  |  |
|         `enabled` | boolean | _unset_ | Set to Some(true) to enable HA for the sequencer. If None it will be enabled if the storage supports replication. |
|         `keep-alive-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `50 milliseconds` | how frequently will we ensure the sequencer watermark is updated to ensure it still appears alive |
|         `offline-duration` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `8 seconds` | how long should a sequencer watermark be lagging for it to be flagged as offline |
|         `online-check-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | how frequently should this sequencer check that nodes are still online |
|         `total-node-count` | int (PositiveInt) | `10` | how many sequencer writers will there ever be in this synchronizer. recommend setting to a value larger than the current topology to allow for expansion. |
|         **connection-pool** |  |  |  |
|             `active-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Time to wait until the first connection in the pool becomes active during failover. |
|             `health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between health checks of the connection pool. |
|             `initial-locked-connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Time to wait until the initial connection is either active or passive (external sequencer does not use this) |
|             `initial-must-remain-active-connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `1 minute` | If using an external sequencer we want to make sure it is always active, unless it is closing. This timeout is the initial time to wait before first becoming active, otherwise it is shutdown. |
|             **connection** |  |  |  |
|                 `client-connection-check-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Interval for client connection checks. Corresponds to the Postgres `client_connection_check_interval` configuration parameter (Postgres &gt;= 14 only). Millisecond granularity is the lowest supported precision. `None` leaves the server default unchanged whereas a Some(0) explicitly disables the check. On macOS with Postgres 14, this must be disabled, since in Postgres 14, this setting requires the non-standard `POLLRDHUP` extension to the `poll` system call, which is only available on Linux. See html for details. |
|                 `connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `10 seconds` | Timeout for requesting a new connection from the underlying database driver in seconds granularity. |
|                 `health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between one health check completed and the next to start. |
|                 `health-check-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Timeout for running a health check in seconds granularity. |
|                 `initial-acquisition-interval` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `200 milliseconds` | Retry intervals during the initial attempts to acquire the lock. |
|                 `initial-acquisition-max-retries` | int | `5` | Maximum number of retries when trying to acquire the lock for the first time before trying to acquire the lock in a background task. |
|                 `keep-alive-count` | int | `5` | TCP keep-alive count, i.e., how many unanswered keep-alive messages required to consider the connection lost. |
|                 `keep-alive-idle` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `10 seconds` | TCP keep-alive idle time, i.e., how long to wait until sending a keep-alive message when idle. |
|                 `keep-alive-interval` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `1 second` | TCP keep-alive interval, i.e., how long to wait until resending an unanswered keep-alive message. |
|                 `max-inconclusive-read-only-checks` | int | `3` | Number of consecutive inconclusive (timed-out or failed) read-only checks tolerated before the connection is considered lost. A single inconclusive check can be due to transient contention, but a persistently inconclusive check is problematic. |
|                 `passive-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | How long to wait between trying to become active. |

|                     `health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between one health check completed and the next to start. |

|     **config** |  |  |  |
|         `availability-dissemination-patience` | duration | `5 seconds` | The amount of time that the availability module waits for a dissemination acknowledgement from a peer before re-sending the batch. If `None`, re-sending is disabled. |
|         `availability-max-non-ordered-batches-per-node` | int | `1000` | The maximum number of unordered batches that the availability module will accept from each peer. The purpose of this limit is to prevent malicious peers from consuming memory and storage by flooding new batches to correct nodes. |
|         `availability-min-proposal-creation-delay` | duration | `250 milliseconds` | The minimum delay between consecutive proposal creations in the availability module. This prevents the node from creating proposals too frequently. |
|         `availability-number-of-attempts-of-downloading-output-fetch-before-warning` | int | `5` | The number of consecutive failed attempts to retrieve data for an ordered batch before the ordering node emits a warning to the logs |
|         `blocking-db-read-timeout` | duration | `1 minute` | The maximum time allowed for block reads to restore state from the database after restarts. Currently, if the timeout is reached during any of the blocking reads on startup, the node shuts down and must restart to try again. |
|         `consensus-block-completion-timeout` | duration | `10 seconds` | The maximum time that nodes will wait for each leader to order the next expected block in the corresponding index. After each block in a segment is ordered (and at the start of each epoch), this timeout is reset for that corresponding segment. If the timeout is reached for a given segment, the node will vote for a View Change. |
|         `consensus-empty-block-creation-timeout` | duration | `500 milliseconds` | The time after which a leader will create an empty block if there is no regular block available to propose, in order to ensure that: (i) View Changes are avoided, and (ii) BFT time advances at a regular frequency. Note that due to (i), it is recommended that this consensusEmptyBlockCreationTimeout be substantially less than the consensusBlockCompletionTimeout to account for latency, retransmissions, etc. |
|         `consensus-enable-flushing-segment` | boolean | `true` | If `true`, when this node detects that a strong quorum of segments are completed while the one it leads is in progress, it will flush the segment and complete all slots in parallel to avoid making other nodes wait for the epoch to complete. This is a performance optimization that can be disabled if issues arise with the flushing logic. |
|         `consensus-enable-log-end-of-epoch-progress` | boolean | `false` | If `true`, logs are emitted to track progress at the end of each epoch with the status of segments from all leaders. |
|         `consensus-flushing-min-blocks` | int | `2` | The minimum number of blocks that must be missing to complete in a segment before the node will be able to decide to flush the segment and complete all slots in parallel if it detects that a strong quorum of segments are completed while the one it leads is in progress. If fewer blocks than this are missing, the node will complete them normally one at a time. |
|         `consensus-new-epoch-topology-warn-timeout` | duration | `2 seconds` | The time that nodes will wait at the start of a new epoch for the topology to be activated. If the timeout is reached, the node will create a warning log to indicate the reason for delay in progress was the topology activation, but the node will continue to wait for the topology to be activated. |
|         `consensus-queue-max-size` | int | `DefaultConsensusQueueMaxSize` ‡ | The maximum size per consensus-related queue. |
|         `consensus-queue-per-node-quota` | int | `1024` | The maximum number of messages per node stored in consensus-related queues (quotas are maintained separately per queue). |
|         `dedicated-execution-context-divisor` | int | `DefaultDedicatedExecutionContextDivisor` ‡ | Optionally, a dedicated execution context for the BFT orderer. When set, the BFT orderer creates an independent execution context, separate from the colocated sequencer, using `Threading.detectNumberOfThreads` / `dedicatedExecutionContextDivisor`. This is potentially useful in deployments with heavy execution context contention between the sequencer and BFT ordering layer. If set to the default None, the BFT orderer shares the same execution context as the sequencer. |
|         `delayed-init-queue-max-size` | int | `1024` | The maximum size of the delayed init queue. This queue is used by modules to save incoming events in memory while the module is still initializing. Once startup is complete, the module processes all events from the delayed init queue first before continuing to read newly received events. |
|         `enable-performance-metrics` | boolean | `true` | If `true`, the BFT layer emits metrics to track real-time and historical performance. |
|         `epoch-state-transfer-future-epoch-queue-max-size` | int | `DefaultConsensusQueueMaxSize` ‡ | The maximum size of future epoch queue. |
|         `epoch-state-transfer-future-epoch-queue-per-node-quota` | int | `1024` | The maximum number of messages per node stored in future epoch queue |
|         `epoch-state-transfer-how-many-future-epochs-to-download-in-parallel` | int (NonNegativeLong) | `5` | The amount of epochs to speculatively download in parallel during State Transfer. Can be 0 to turn off speculative downloading |
|         `epoch-state-transfer-retry-timeout` | duration | `4 seconds` | The state transfer retry timeout covering periods from requesting blocks from a single epoch up to receiving all the corresponding batches. |
|         `epoch-state-transfer-timeout-for-future-epoch` | duration | `500 milliseconds` | A timeout for how long we will wait if we already potentially have all blocks due to speculatively downloaded an epoch before we make a new network request. This timeout should be quite short since it is only accounting for local processing. |
|         `init-query-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `DefaultInitQueryTimeout` ‡ | The maximum time allowed for individual queries during BFT block orderer initialization (e.g., topology or state queries). Must be less than or equal to `initTimeout`. |
|         `init-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `DefaultInitTimeout` ‡ | The maximum total time allowed for the BFT block orderer to complete initialization, including all startup queries and state restoration. If exceeded, the node fails to start. |
|         `manual-vacuum-enabled` | boolean | `false` | If `true`, the BFT block orderer will attempt to manually vacuum each partition after it becomes cold. |
|         `max-batch-creation-interval` | duration | `100 milliseconds` | The maximum amount of time the mempool will wait for incoming requests before creating and sending a batch to the availability module. |
|         `max-mempool-queue-size` | int | `DefaultMaxMempoolQueueSize` ‡ | The maximum number of pending requests that will be held in the in-memory mempool. Once this queue size is reached, subsequent requests are rejected with a mempool overloaded error. |
|         `min-requests-in-batch` | int | `3` | When the mempool does not create and send a batch due to other factors (see maxBatchCreationInterval), this is the number of requests that the mempool will wait for before shipping a batch to the availability module. |
|         `network-send-attempts` | int (PositiveInt) | `5` | The maximum number of gRPC message send attempts before dropping the message. |
|         `network-send-retry-jitter-cap` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `DefaultNetworkSendRetryMaximumDelay` ‡ | The maximum jittered delay that a node will use to wait between consecutive gRPC message send attempts. The minimum delay is added to the jittered delay. |
|         `network-send-retry-minimum-delay` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `DefaultNetworkSendRetryMinimumDelay` ‡ | The minimum delay between consecutive gRPC message send attempts. This prevents a node from overwhelming peers with rapid successive gRPC message sends, for example if the jitter returns very low values. |
|         `output-enqueue-max-retries` | int | `DefaultOutputEnqueueMaxRetries` ‡ | The maximum number of retry attempts when enqueuing ordered output blocks for delivery to the sequencer core. |
|         `output-enqueue-max-retry-delay` | duration | `5 seconds` | The maximum delay between retry attempts when enqueuing ordered output blocks for delivery to the sequencer core. |
|         `output-fetch-how-many-recipients` | int (PositiveInt) | `2` | The number of recipients that a batch will be attempted to be fetched from in parallel. |
|         `output-fetch-minimum-delay` | duration | `200 milliseconds` | The minimum delay between consecutive output fetch attempts. This prevents a node from overwhelming peers with rapid successive fetch requests, for example if the jitter returns very low values. |
|         `output-fetch-timeout` | duration | `200 milliseconds` | The baseline timeout that a node's availability module will wait while fetching batch data from a peer before giving up and trying a different peer. Timeouts for subsequent fetch attempts are increased via exponential backoff and random jitter between 0 and the computed value. The unit is important, as the exponential is computed on the non-converted value. |
|         `output-fetch-timeout-cap` | duration | `200 milliseconds` | The maximum jittered duration that a node's availability module will wait while fetching batch data from a peer. The minimum delay is added to the jittered delay. |
|         `output-size-of-chunk-of-epochs-to-load-at-start` | int | `10` | The number of epochs to load per chunk when restoring output state from the database on startup. Larger values may speed up startup but consume more memory. |
|         `segment-length-for-pv-34` | int | _unset_ | Optionally, the number of blocks per segment (only taken into account in Protocol Version 34). If set to None, the default epoch length is used. |
|         `send-blacklist-ttl` | duration | `3 minutes` | The time-to-live for blacklisting randomly chosen peers that have failed to respond to a request as part of a workflow (e.g., batches fetching or a state transfer session). After this time has elapsed, the peer is removed from the blacklist and can be re-contacted. |
|         `view-change-timeout-override` | duration | _unset_ | Optionally, the base duration to use for view change timeouts for ISS segments. If specified, this value will be used instead of the pbftViewChangeTimeout set in the network-wide SequencingParameters topology transaction. Note that this value is not persisted (in-memory only), and must be set back to None to return using the SequencingParameters value. |
|         **batch-aggregator** |  |  |  |
|             `maximum-batch-size` | int (PositiveNumeric) | `500` | Maximum number of queries in a batch. |
|             `type` | one of `batching`, `no-batching` | **required** | Choose one type — see [BatchAggregatorConfig](#batchaggregatorconfig). Parameters for that batcher that batches queries (e.g., to a DB). |
|         **batch-aggregator** `type = batching` |  |  |  |
|             `maximum-in-flight` | int (PositiveNumeric) | `2` | Maximum number of in-flight get queries. |
|         **batch-aggregator** `type = no-batching` |  |  |  |
|             `max-parallel-batches` | int (PositiveNumeric) | `2` | Maximum number of batches to execute in parallel when using runMany. |
|         **initial-network** |  |  |  |
|             `overwrite-stored-endpoints` | boolean | `false` | whether the peers in storage should be replaced by this config's peers endpoint on startup |
|             `peer-endpoints` | array of P2PEndpointConfig | `[]` | set of peers that the local node expects to connect to on startup |
|             **connection-management-config** |  |  |  |
|                 `connection-retry-delay-multiplier` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `NonNegativeInt.two` ‡ | The backoff factor applied to the delay between subsequent failed retry attempts |
|                 `flow-control-buffer` | int (PositiveInt) | `128` | If set, enables P2P flow control on the sender side with the specified buffer size. If not set, the gRPC implementation will manage send buffers in case of slow receivers (and may OOM). |
|                 `flow-control-buffer-drop-newest` | boolean | `false` | If true, newest excess sends, rather than oldest, will be dropped from the flow control buffer. |
|                 `flow-control-ready-allowance` | int (PositiveInt) | `PositiveInt.one` ‡ | If `flowControlBuffer` is set, a "ready to send" signal will be valid for the specified number of messages without further checks. |
|                 `initial-connection-max-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `500 milliseconds` | The base max delay between retry attempts, used to bootstrap the jitter mechanism |
|                 `initial-connection-retry-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `500 milliseconds` | The base delay between retry attempts when connecting to a peer |
|                 `max-connection-attempts-before-warning` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `30` | The number of failed connection attempts after which a WARN log is emitted |
|                 `max-connection-retry-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `2 minutes` | The maximum delay between retry attempts to connect to a peer |
|             **endpoint-authentication** |  |  |  |
|                 `enabled` | boolean | `true` | Whether authentication should be setup between peers. For security reasons, this should NOT be disabled in any real production deployments, unless a decision is explicitly made to exclusively rely on mTLS for peer-to-peer authentication. |
|                 **auth-token** |  |  |  |

|                     `refresh-auth-token-before-expiry` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `20 seconds` | how much time before the auth token expires should we fetch a new one? |

|                     `flow-control-window` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ | Switches to manual gRPC flow control and sets its window; if `None`, then it is not configured and the implementation default is used. At most one of `flowControlWindow` and `initialFlowControlWindow` can be set. |
|                     `initial-flow-control-window` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ | Switches to automatic gRPC flow control and sets its initial window; if `None`, then it is not configured and the implementation default is used. At most one of `flowControlWindow` and `initialFlowControlWindow` can be set. |
|                     `max-inbound-message-size` | int (NonNegativeInt) | **required** |  |
|                     `trace-context-propagation` | one of `enabled`, `disabled` | **required** |  |
|                     **keep-alive-client** |  |  |  |
|                         `idle-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `30 minutes` | If there are no RPCs during this duration, the channel will close all connections and switch to Idle state (default is 30 min) |
|                         `keep-alive-without-calls` | boolean | `false` | Enables sending of keep alive even when there are no RPCs on the channel (default to false). This increases network resource consumption for idle connections and requires the server to enable permitKeepAliveWithoutCalls. Prefer using idleTimeout. |
|                         `time` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `40 seconds` | Sets the time without read activity before sending a keepalive ping. Do not set to small numbers (default is 40s) |
|                         `timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Sets the time waiting for read activity after sending a keepalive ping (default is 15s). The default timeout was previously 20s and was lowered to 15s, because besides configuring the gRPC KeepAliveManager, it also sets up the socket's TCP_USER_TIMEOUT (see html). 15s gives a larger margin to detect a faulty connection earlier and retry a submission on another sequencer via amplification, thereby avoiding a request failure. |

|         **storage** |  |  |  |
|             `type` | one of `memory`, `h2`, `postgres` | `memory` † | Choose one type — see [StorageConfig](#storageconfig). Determines how a node stores persistent data. |
|             **parameters** |  |  |  |
|                 `connection-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | How long to wait for acquiring a database connection |
|                 `fail-fast-on-startup` | boolean | `true` | If true, the node will fail-fast when the database cannot be connected to If false, the node will wait indefinitely for the database to come up |
|                 `failed-to-fatal-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 minutes` | Delay after which, if the storage is continuously in a Failed state, it will escalate to Fatal. The default value is 5 minutes. Components that use the storage as a health dependency can then determine how to react. Currently, the sequencer declares it as a fatal dependency for its liveness health, which means it will transition to NOT_SERVING if this delay is exceeded, allowing a monitoring infrastructure to restart it. **NOTE**: Currently this only applies to DbStorageSingle, which is only used by the sequencer. TODO(i24240): Apply the same behavior to `DbStorageMulti` |
|                 `max-connections` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ | Allows for setting the maximum number of db connections used by Canton and the ledger API server. If None, the value will be auto-detected from the number of processors. Has no effect, if the number of connections is already set via slick options (i.e., `config.numThreads`). |
|                 `migrate-and-start` | boolean | `false` | if true, db migrations will be applied to the database (default is to abort start if db migrates are pending to force an explicit upgrade) |
|                 `migrations-paths` | array of string | `[]` | Where should database migrations be read from. Enables specialized DDL for different database servers (e.g. Postgres). |
|                 `repeatable-migrations-paths` | array of string | `[]` | Additional locations to scan for migrations. The goal is to be able to provide additional database table settings under this path, i.e. vacuum/analyze settings for Postgres. The paths should only contain repeatable migrations (files like `R__table_settings.sql`). |
|                 `unsafe-baseline-on-migrate` | boolean | `false` | TO BE USED ONLY FOR TESTING! &lt;p&gt;Whether to automatically call baseline when migrate is executed against a non-empty schema with no schema history table. This schema will then be baselined with the \{@code baselineVersion\} before executing the migrations. Only migrations above \{@code baselineVersion\} will then be applied.&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;This is useful for databases projects where the initial vendor schema is not empty&lt;/p&gt; If baseline should be called on migrate for non-empty schemas, \{ @code false\} if not. (default: \{ @code false\}) |
|                 `unsafe-clean-on-validation-error` | boolean | `false` | TO BE USED ONLY FOR TESTING! Clean the database if validation during DB migration fails. |
|                 `warn-on-slow-query` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | _unset_ | Optional time when we start logging a query as slow. |
|                 `warn-on-slow-query-interval` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | How often to repeat the logging statement for slow queries. |
|                 **connection-allocation** |  |  |  |
|                     `num-ledger-api` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ |  |
|                     `num-reads` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ |  |
|                     `num-writes` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ |  |
|                 **partitions** |  |  |  |
|                     `initial-bft-orderer-tables-partition-size` | int | `1500` | Initial partition size for bft-orderer tables. Note that this config is only read once, during the initial database setup and later changes to this value won't have any effect. This is also only used in Postgres setups. |

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.storage`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.storage {
  parameters {
    connection-allocation {
      # num-ledger-api = <int>
      # num-reads = <int>
      # num-writes = <int>
    }
    connection-timeout = "5 seconds"
    fail-fast-on-startup = true
    failed-to-fatal-delay = "5 minutes"
    # max-connections = <int>
    migrate-and-start = false
    migrations-paths = [ ]
    partitions {
      initial-bft-orderer-tables-partition-size = 1500
    }
    repeatable-migrations-paths = [ ]
    unsafe-baseline-on-migrate = false
    unsafe-clean-on-validation-error = false
    # warn-on-slow-query = <duration>
    warn-on-slow-query-interval = "5 seconds"
  }
  type = memory|h2|postgres   # required
}
```

`` supports these types: `memory`, `h2`, `postgres`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.storage {
  type = memory
  parameters {
    connection-allocation {
      # num-ledger-api = <int>
      # num-reads = <int>
      # num-writes = <int>
    }
    connection-timeout = "5 seconds"
    fail-fast-on-startup = true
    failed-to-fatal-delay = "5 minutes"
    # max-connections = <int>
    migrate-and-start = false
    migrations-paths = [ ]
    partitions {
      initial-bft-orderer-tables-partition-size = 1500
    }
    repeatable-migrations-paths = [ ]
    unsafe-baseline-on-migrate = false
    unsafe-clean-on-validation-error = false
    # warn-on-slow-query = <duration>
    warn-on-slow-query-interval = "5 seconds"
  }
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.storage {
  type = h2
  parameters {
    connection-allocation {
      # num-ledger-api = <int>
      # num-reads = <int>
      # num-writes = <int>
    }
    connection-timeout = "5 seconds"
    fail-fast-on-startup = true
    failed-to-fatal-delay = "5 minutes"
    # max-connections = <int>
    migrate-and-start = false
    migrations-paths = [ ]
    partitions {
      initial-bft-orderer-tables-partition-size = 1500
    }
    repeatable-migrations-paths = [ ]
    unsafe-baseline-on-migrate = false
    unsafe-clean-on-validation-error = false
    # warn-on-slow-query = <duration>
    warn-on-slow-query-interval = "5 seconds"
  }

```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.storage {
  type = postgres
  parameters {
    connection-allocation {
      # num-ledger-api = <int>
      # num-reads = <int>
      # num-writes = <int>
    }
    connection-timeout = "5 seconds"
    fail-fast-on-startup = true
    failed-to-fatal-delay = "5 minutes"
    # max-connections = <int>
    migrate-and-start = false
    migrations-paths = [ ]
    partitions {
      initial-bft-orderer-tables-partition-size = 1500
    }
    repeatable-migrations-paths = [ ]
    unsafe-baseline-on-migrate = false
    unsafe-clean-on-validation-error = false
    # warn-on-slow-query = <duration>

  }
```

</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type` | one of `memory`, `h2`, `postgres` | `memory` † | Choose one type — see [StorageConfig](#storageconfig). Determines how a node stores persistent data. |
| **parameters** |  |  |  |
|     `connection-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` (in practice `30 seconds`) ◊ | How long to wait for acquiring a database connection |
|     `fail-fast-on-startup` | boolean | `true` | If true, the node will fail-fast when the database cannot be connected to If false, the node will wait indefinitely for the database to come up |
|     `failed-to-fatal-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 minutes` | Delay after which, if the storage is continuously in a Failed state, it will escalate to Fatal. The default value is 5 minutes. Components that use the storage as a health dependency can then determine how to react. Currently, the sequencer declares it as a fatal dependency for its liveness health, which means it will transition to NOT_SERVING if this delay is exceeded, allowing a monitoring infrastructure to restart it. **NOTE**: Currently this only applies to DbStorageSingle, which is only used by the sequencer. TODO(i24240): Apply the same behavior to `DbStorageMulti` |
|     `max-connections` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ | Allows for setting the maximum number of db connections used by Canton and the ledger API server. If None, the value will be auto-detected from the number of processors. Has no effect, if the number of connections is already set via slick options (i.e., `config.numThreads`). |
|     `migrate-and-start` | boolean | `false` | if true, db migrations will be applied to the database (default is to abort start if db migrates are pending to force an explicit upgrade) |
|     `migrations-paths` | array of string | `[]` | Where should database migrations be read from. Enables specialized DDL for different database servers (e.g. Postgres). |
|     `repeatable-migrations-paths` | array of string | `[]` | Additional locations to scan for migrations. The goal is to be able to provide additional database table settings under this path, i.e. vacuum/analyze settings for Postgres. The paths should only contain repeatable migrations (files like `R__table_settings.sql`). |
|     `unsafe-baseline-on-migrate` | boolean | `false` | TO BE USED ONLY FOR TESTING! &lt;p&gt;Whether to automatically call baseline when migrate is executed against a non-empty schema with no schema history table. This schema will then be baselined with the \{@code baselineVersion\} before executing the migrations. Only migrations above \{@code baselineVersion\} will then be applied.&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;This is useful for databases projects where the initial vendor schema is not empty&lt;/p&gt; If baseline should be called on migrate for non-empty schemas, \{ @code false\} if not. (default: \{ @code false\}) |
|     `unsafe-clean-on-validation-error` | boolean | `false` | TO BE USED ONLY FOR TESTING! Clean the database if validation during DB migration fails. |
|     `warn-on-slow-query` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | _unset_ | Optional time when we start logging a query as slow. |
|     `warn-on-slow-query-interval` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | How often to repeat the logging statement for slow queries. |
|     **connection-allocation** |  |  |  |
|         `num-ledger-api` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ |  |
|         `num-reads` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ |  |
|         `num-writes` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ |  |
|     **partitions** |  |  |  |
|         `initial-bft-orderer-tables-partition-size` | int | `1500` | Initial partition size for bft-orderer tables. Note that this config is only read once, during the initial database setup and later changes to this value won't have any effect. This is also only used in Postgres setups. |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.time-tracker`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.time-tracker {
  min-observation-duration = "24 hours"
  observation-latency = "250 milliseconds"
  patience-duration = "500 milliseconds"
  time-request {
    initial-retry-delay = "200 milliseconds"
    max-retry-delay = "5 seconds"
    max-sequencing-delay = "10 seconds"
  }
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `min-observation-duration` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `24 hours` | We will try to ensure that we receive a time at least once during this duration (measured using the host clock). This is practically useful if there is no other activity on the synchronizer as the sequencer client will then have an event to acknowledge allowing sequenced events to be pruned before this point. We may in the future use this to monitor clock skews between the host and synchronizer. |
| `observation-latency` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `250 milliseconds` | Even if the host and synchronizer clocks are perfectly synchronized there will always be some latency for an event to be delivered (storage, transmission, processing). If the current host time exceeds the next expected timestamp by this observation latency then we will request a time proof (unless we have received a recent event within the patience duration described below). This parameter will be ignored, if the underlying node uses a SimClock. |
| `patience-duration` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `500 milliseconds` | We will only request a time proof if this given duration has elapsed since we last received an event (measured using the host clock). This prevents requesting timestamps when we are observing events from the synchronizer (particularly if the local node is catching up on old activity). |
| **time-request** |  |  |  |
|     `initial-retry-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `200 milliseconds` | The initial retry delay if the request to send a sequenced event fails |
|     `max-retry-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | The max retry delay if the request to send a sequenced event fails |
|     `max-sequencing-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `10 seconds` | If our request for a sequenced event was successful, how long should we wait to observe it from the sequencer before starting a new request. |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.topology`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.topology {
  broadcast-batch-size = 100
  broadcast-retry-delay = "10 seconds"
  disable-optional-topology-checks = false
  dispatch-queue-backpressure-limit = 100
  enable-topology-state-cache-consistency-checks = false
  max-topology-state-cache-items = 10000
  topology-state-cache-eviction-threshold = 250
  topology-transaction-observation-timeout = "30 seconds"
  topology-transaction-registration-timeout = "20 seconds"
  use-time-proofs-to-observe-effective-time = false
  validate-initial-topology-snapshot = true
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `broadcast-batch-size` | int (PositiveInt) | `100` | The maximum number of topology transactions sent in a topology transaction broadcast |
| `broadcast-retry-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `10 seconds` | The delay after which a failed dispatch cycle will be triggered again. |
| `disable-optional-topology-checks` | boolean | `false` | if true (default is false), don't run the optional checks which prevent accidental damage to this node |
| `dispatch-queue-backpressure-limit` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `100` | new topology requests will be backpressured if the number of existing requests exceeds this number |
| `enable-topology-state-cache-consistency-checks` | boolean | `false` | If true, the topology state cache runs additional consistency checks. This is costly and should not be enabled in production environments. |
| `max-topology-state-cache-items` | int (PositiveInt) | `10000` | The maximum number of (UIDs + namespaces, transaction types) that can be cached in the write through cache |
| `topology-state-cache-eviction-threshold` | int (PositiveInt) | `250` | How far should the topology cache be "emptied" during an eviction run |
| `topology-transaction-observation-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `30 seconds` | Determines up to how long the node waits for observing the dispatched topology transactions in its own local synchronizer store. The observation timeout is checked against the node's wall clock. After this timeout, the node fails the dispatch cycle. This timeout is only triggered, if the sequencer accepts the topology transaction broadcast submission request, but drops the message during ordering (for whatever reason). |
| `topology-transaction-registration-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `20 seconds` | Used to determine the max sequencing time for topology transaction broadcasts. |
| `use-time-proofs-to-observe-effective-time` | boolean | `false` | Whether the node will use time proofs to observe when an effective time has been reached. If false, no time proofs will be sent to the sequencers by any Canton node. |
| `validate-initial-topology-snapshot` | boolean | `true` | if false, the validation is skipped and the snapshot is directly imported. this is risky as it might create a fork if the validation was changed. therefore, we only use this with great care. the proper solution is to make validation so fast that it doesn't impact performance. |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.traffic-config`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.traffic-config {
  batch-aggregator-config {
    maximum-batch-size = 500
    type = batching|no-batching   # required
  }
  maximum-traffic-consumed-cache-size = 1000
  maximum-traffic-purchased-cache-size = 1000
  pruning-retention-window = "2 hours"
  submission-timestamp-in-future-tolerance = "5 seconds"
  traffic-consumed-cache-ttl = "2 hours"
  traffic-purchased-cache-size-per-member = 3
}
```

`batch-aggregator-config` supports these types: `batching`, `no-batching`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.traffic-config.batch-aggregator-config {
  type = batching
  maximum-batch-size = 500
  maximum-in-flight = 2
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.traffic-config.batch-aggregator-config {
  type = no-batching
  max-parallel-batches = 2
  maximum-batch-size = 500
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `maximum-traffic-consumed-cache-size` | int (PositiveInt) | `1000` | Maximum number of entries (members) to keep in the traffic consumed cache. |
| `maximum-traffic-purchased-cache-size` | int (PositiveInt) | `1000` | The maximum number of entries (= members) to keep in the cache. |
| `pruning-retention-window` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `2 hours` | the duration for which balances are kept in the cache and the store. Balances older than this duration will be pruned at regular intervals. |
| `submission-timestamp-in-future-tolerance` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | the tolerance window that should be added to future dated submission timestamp that can still be accepted by this sequencer. |
| `traffic-consumed-cache-ttl` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `2 hours` | the duration for which consumed traffic entries are kept in the cache after the last time they've been accessed. |
| `traffic-purchased-cache-size-per-member` | int (PositiveInt) | `3` | How many traffic purchased entries to keep in memory for each member. |
| **batch-aggregator-config** |  |  |  |
|     `maximum-batch-size` | int (PositiveNumeric) | `500` | Maximum number of queries in a batch. |
|     `type` | one of `batching`, `no-batching` | `batching` † | Choose one type — see [BatchAggregatorConfig](#batchaggregatorconfig). Parameters for that batcher that batches queries (e.g., to a DB). |
| **batch-aggregator-config** `type = batching` |  |  |  |
|     `maximum-in-flight` | int (PositiveNumeric) | `2` | Maximum number of in-flight get queries. |
| **batch-aggregator-config** `type = no-batching` |  |  |  |
|     `max-parallel-batches` | int (PositiveNumeric) | `2` | Maximum number of batches to execute in parallel when using runMany. |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

## Configuration types

Sections under this page that take a `type`, and the keys each type accepts. A key belonging to another type fails startup.

### AuthServiceConfig

Set `type` to one of `wildcard`, `unsafe-jwt-hmac-256`, `jwt-rs-256-crt`, `jwt-es-256-crt`, `jwt-es-512-crt`, `jwt-jwks`, `party-jwt`.

Occurs at:

- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api.auth-services[]`

#### `type = wildcard`

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api.auth-services = [
  {
    type = wildcard
    # takes no further keys
  }
]
```

#### `type = unsafe-jwt-hmac-256`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api.auth-services = [
  {
    type = unsafe-jwt-hmac-256
    access-level = wildcard
    # max-token-life = <duration>
    privileged = false
    secret = "..."   # required
    # target-audience = <string>
    # target-scope = <string>
    users = [
      {
        allowed-services = [ ]   # required
        user-id = "..."   # required
      }
    ]
  }
]
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `access-level` | one of `admin`, `wildcard` | `wildcard` |  |
| `max-token-life` | duration (NonNegativeDuration) | `NonNegativeDuration(Duration.Inf)` ‡ |  |
| `privileged` | boolean | `false` |  |
| `secret` | string (NonEmptyString) | **required** |  |
| `target-audience` | string | _unset_ |  |
| `target-scope` | string | _unset_ |  |
| `users` | array of AuthorizedUser | `[]` |  |
| `users[].allowed-services` | array of string | **required** |  |
| `users[].user-id` | string | **required** |  |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

#### `type = jwt-rs-256-crt`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
    # max-token-life = <duration>
    privileged = false
    # target-audience = <string>
    # target-scope = <string>

  }
]
```

### BatchAggregatorConfig

Set `type` to one of `batching`, `no-batching`.

Occurs at:

- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.parameters.batching.aggregator`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.parameters.batching.contract-store-aggregator`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.parameters.batching.mediator-fetch-finalized-responses-aggregator`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.parameters.batching.mediator-store-finalized-responses-aggregator`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.parameters.batching.topology-cache-aggregator`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.config.batch-aggregator`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.traffic-config.batch-aggregator-config`

#### `type = batching`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.traffic-config.batch-aggregator-config {
  type = batching
  maximum-batch-size = 500
  maximum-in-flight = 2
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `maximum-batch-size` | int (PositiveNumeric) | `500` | Maximum number of queries in a batch. |
| `maximum-in-flight` | int (PositiveNumeric) | `2` | Maximum number of in-flight get queries. |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

#### `type = no-batching`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.traffic-config.batch-aggregator-config {
  type = no-batching
  max-parallel-batches = 2
  maximum-batch-size = 500
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max-parallel-batches` | int (PositiveNumeric) | `2` | Maximum number of batches to execute in parallel when using runMany. |
| `maximum-batch-size` | int (PositiveNumeric) | `500` | Maximum number of queries in a batch. |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### EncryptedPrivateStoreConfig

Set `type` to one of `kms`.

Occurs at:

- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto.private-key-store.encryption`

#### `type = kms`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto.private-key-store.encryption {
  type = kms
  reverted = false
  # wrapper-key-id = <string>
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `reverted` | boolean | `false` | when true decrypts the stored private keys and stores them in clear, disabling the encrypted crypto private key store in the process |
| `wrapper-key-id` | string (KmsKeyId) | _unset_ | defines key identifier for the wrapper key (e.g. ARN for AWS SDK). When it's None Canton will either create a new key or use a previous existent key |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### FiniteDurationDistribution

Set `type` to one of `constant-distribution`, `weighted-distribution`, `linear-distribution`, `power-distribution`.

Occurs at:

- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.config.standalone.test-slowdown.post-ordering-delay`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.config.standalone.test-slowdown.send-delay.default-delay-distribution`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.config.standalone.test-slowdown.send-delay.delays-by-recipients[].delay-distribution`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.config.standalone.test-slowdown.topology-delay.get-ordering-topology-delay`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.config.standalone.test-slowdown.topology-delay.request-inspection-delay`

#### `type = constant-distribution`

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.config.standalone.test-slowdown.post-ordering-delay {

  duration = "30 seconds"   # required
}
```

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `duration` | duration | **required** |  |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

```hocon
}
```

| Key | Type | Default | Description |

| `weighted-durations` | array of WeightedDuration | **required** |  |

| `weighted-durations[].weight` | int (PositiveInt) | **required** |  |

### IdentityConfig

Set `type` to one of `external`, `auto`, `manual`.

Occurs at:

- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.init.identity`

#### `type = external`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.init.identity {
  type = external
  certificates = [ ]
  identifier = "..."   # required
  # namespace = <string>
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `certificates` | array of File | `[]` | optional certificates to use (in case we have external root keys) |
| `identifier` | string | **required** | the string to use as identifier of the node |
| `namespace` | string | _unset_ | optional fingerprint to use as the namespace (default is to extract it from the first certificate) |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

#### `type = auto`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.init.identity {
  type = auto
  identifier {
    type = random|explicit|config   # required
  }
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

### KmsConfig

Set `type` to one of `driver`, `aws`, `gcp`.

Occurs at:

- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto.kms`

#### `type = driver`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto.kms {
  type = driver
  config = { }   # required
  health-check-period = "10 seconds"
  name = "..."   # required
  retries {
    create-key-check {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
    failures {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
  }
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `config` | config-value | **required** | The driver specific raw config section |
| `health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `10 seconds` | How long to wait between health checks of the KMS driver |
| `name` | string | **required** | The name of the external KMS driver |
| `retries.create-key-check.initial-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | **required** | initial delay before the first retry |
| `retries.create-key-check.max-delay` | duration (NonNegativeDuration) | **required** | max delay between retries |
| `retries.create-key-check.max-retries` | int | **required** | max number of retries |
| `retries.failures.initial-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | **required** | initial delay before the first retry |
| `retries.failures.max-delay` | duration (NonNegativeDuration) | **required** | max delay between retries |
| `retries.failures.max-retries` | int | **required** | max number of retries |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto.kms {

  region = "..."   # required

    create-key-check {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
    failures {

      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
  }
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `audit-logging` | boolean | `false` | when enabled, all calls to KMS will be logged. Defaults to false. |
| `custom-tags.<custom-tag>` | string | `[]` | custom tags to attach to AWS KMS keys created by Canton. |
| `disable-ssl-verification` | boolean | `false` | When set to true, SSL verification is disabled. Mostly for testing purposes. Can only be used if non-standard-config is enabled. |
| `endpoint-override` | string | _unset_ | the [optional] endpoint for a proxy to be used by the KMS client. |
| `multi-region-key` | boolean | `false` | flag to enable multiRegion keys (Canton will generate single region keys by default) |
| `region` | string | **required** | defines the AWS region to be use (e.g. us-east-1) |
| `retries.create-key-check.initial-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | **required** | initial delay before the first retry |
| `retries.create-key-check.max-delay` | duration (NonNegativeDuration) | **required** | max delay between retries |
| `retries.create-key-check.max-retries` | int | **required** | max number of retries |
| `retries.failures.initial-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | **required** | initial delay before the first retry |
| `retries.failures.max-delay` | duration (NonNegativeDuration) | **required** | max delay between retries |
| `retries.failures.max-retries` | int | **required** | max number of retries |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### SequencerConfig

Set `type` to one of `database`, `external`, `bft-sequencer`.

Occurs at:

- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer`

#### `type = database`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer {
  type = database
  high-availability {
    connection-pool {
      active-timeout = "15 seconds"
      connection {
        client-connection-check-interval = "5 seconds"
        connection-timeout = "10 seconds"
        health-check-period = "5 seconds"
        health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
        initial-acquisition-interval = "200 milliseconds"
        initial-acquisition-max-retries = 5
        keep-alive-count = 5
        keep-alive-idle = "10 seconds"
        keep-alive-interval = "1 second"
        lock {
          health-check-period = "5 seconds"
          health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
        }
        max-inconclusive-read-only-checks = 3
        passive-check-period = "15 seconds"
      }
      health-check-period = "5 seconds"
      initial-locked-connection-timeout = "15 seconds"
      initial-must-remain-active-connection-timeout = "1 minute"
    }
    # enabled = <boolean>
    exclusive-storage {
      connection-pool {
        active-timeout = "15 seconds"
        connection {
          client-connection-check-interval = "5 seconds"
          connection-timeout = "10 seconds"
          health-check-period = "5 seconds"
          health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
          initial-acquisition-interval = "200 milliseconds"
          initial-acquisition-max-retries = 5
          keep-alive-count = 5
          keep-alive-idle = "10 seconds"
          keep-alive-interval = "1 second"
          lock {
            health-check-period = "5 seconds"
            health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
          }
          max-inconclusive-read-only-checks = 3
          passive-check-period = "15 seconds"
        }
        health-check-period = "5 seconds"
        initial-locked-connection-timeout = "15 seconds"
        initial-must-remain-active-connection-timeout = "1 minute"
      }
      max-connections = 2
    }
    keep-alive-interval = "50 milliseconds"
    offline-duration = "8 seconds"
    online-check-interval = "5 seconds"
    total-node-count = 10
  }
  pruning {
    max-pruning-batch-size = 50000
    # pruning-metric-update-interval = <duration>
    traffic-purchased-retention = "1 hour"
  }
  reader {
    checkpoint-interval = "5 seconds"
    event-generation-parallelism = 4
    payload-batch-size = 10
    payload-batch-window = "5 milliseconds"
    payload-fetch-parallelism = 2
    # polling-interval = <duration>
    read-batch-size = 100
  }
  # testing-interceptor = <string>
  writer {
    type = low-latency|high-throughput   # required
  }
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `high-availability.connection-pool.active-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Time to wait until the first connection in the pool becomes active during failover. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.client-connection-check-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Interval for client connection checks. Corresponds to the Postgres `client_connection_check_interval` configuration parameter (Postgres &gt;= 14 only). Millisecond granularity is the lowest supported precision. `None` leaves the server default unchanged whereas a Some(0) explicitly disables the check. On macOS with Postgres 14, this must be disabled, since in Postgres 14, this setting requires the non-standard `POLLRDHUP` extension to the `poll` system call, which is only available on Linux. See html for details. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `10 seconds` | Timeout for requesting a new connection from the underlying database driver in seconds granularity. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between one health check completed and the next to start. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.health-check-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Timeout for running a health check in seconds granularity. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.initial-acquisition-interval` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `200 milliseconds` | Retry intervals during the initial attempts to acquire the lock. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.initial-acquisition-max-retries` | int | `5` | Maximum number of retries when trying to acquire the lock for the first time before trying to acquire the lock in a background task. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.keep-alive-count` | int | `5` | TCP keep-alive count, i.e., how many unanswered keep-alive messages required to consider the connection lost. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.keep-alive-idle` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `10 seconds` | TCP keep-alive idle time, i.e., how long to wait until sending a keep-alive message when idle. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.keep-alive-interval` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `1 second` | TCP keep-alive interval, i.e., how long to wait until resending an unanswered keep-alive message. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.lock.health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between one health check completed and the next to start. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.lock.health-check-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Timeout for running a health check in seconds granularity. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.max-inconclusive-read-only-checks` | int | `3` | Number of consecutive inconclusive (timed-out or failed) read-only checks tolerated before the connection is considered lost. A single inconclusive check can be due to transient contention, but a persistently inconclusive check is problematic. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.connection.passive-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | How long to wait between trying to become active. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between health checks of the connection pool. |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.initial-locked-connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Time to wait until the initial connection is either active or passive (external sequencer does not use this) |
| `high-availability.connection-pool.initial-must-remain-active-connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `1 minute` | If using an external sequencer we want to make sure it is always active, unless it is closing. This timeout is the initial time to wait before first becoming active, otherwise it is shutdown. |
| `high-availability.enabled` | boolean | _unset_ | Set to Some(true) to enable HA for the sequencer. If None it will be enabled if the storage supports replication. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.active-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Time to wait until the first connection in the pool becomes active during failover. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.client-connection-check-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Interval for client connection checks. Corresponds to the Postgres `client_connection_check_interval` configuration parameter (Postgres &gt;= 14 only). Millisecond granularity is the lowest supported precision. `None` leaves the server default unchanged whereas a Some(0) explicitly disables the check. On macOS with Postgres 14, this must be disabled, since in Postgres 14, this setting requires the non-standard `POLLRDHUP` extension to the `poll` system call, which is only available on Linux. See html for details. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `10 seconds` | Timeout for requesting a new connection from the underlying database driver in seconds granularity. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between one health check completed and the next to start. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.health-check-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Timeout for running a health check in seconds granularity. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.initial-acquisition-interval` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `200 milliseconds` | Retry intervals during the initial attempts to acquire the lock. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.initial-acquisition-max-retries` | int | `5` | Maximum number of retries when trying to acquire the lock for the first time before trying to acquire the lock in a background task. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.keep-alive-count` | int | `5` | TCP keep-alive count, i.e., how many unanswered keep-alive messages required to consider the connection lost. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.keep-alive-idle` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `10 seconds` | TCP keep-alive idle time, i.e., how long to wait until sending a keep-alive message when idle. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.keep-alive-interval` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `1 second` | TCP keep-alive interval, i.e., how long to wait until resending an unanswered keep-alive message. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.lock.health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between one health check completed and the next to start. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.lock.health-check-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Timeout for running a health check in seconds granularity. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.max-inconclusive-read-only-checks` | int | `3` | Number of consecutive inconclusive (timed-out or failed) read-only checks tolerated before the connection is considered lost. A single inconclusive check can be due to transient contention, but a persistently inconclusive check is problematic. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.connection.passive-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | How long to wait between trying to become active. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between health checks of the connection pool. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.initial-locked-connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Time to wait until the initial connection is either active or passive (external sequencer does not use this) |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.connection-pool.initial-must-remain-active-connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `1 minute` | If using an external sequencer we want to make sure it is always active, unless it is closing. This timeout is the initial time to wait before first becoming active, otherwise it is shutdown. |
| `high-availability.exclusive-storage.max-connections` | int (PositiveInt) | `2` | maximum read plus write connections. |
| `high-availability.keep-alive-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `50 milliseconds` | how frequently will we ensure the sequencer watermark is updated to ensure it still appears alive |
| `high-availability.offline-duration` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `8 seconds` | how long should a sequencer watermark be lagging for it to be flagged as offline |
| `high-availability.online-check-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | how frequently should this sequencer check that nodes are still online |
| `high-availability.total-node-count` | int (PositiveInt) | `10` | how many sequencer writers will there ever be in this synchronizer. recommend setting to a value larger than the current topology to allow for expansion. |
| `pruning.max-pruning-batch-size` | int (PositiveInt) | `50000` | Maximum number of events to prune from a sequencer at a time, used to break up batches internally |
| `pruning.pruning-metric-update-interval` | duration (PositiveDurationSeconds) | `PositiveDurationSeconds.ofHours(1L).some` ‡ | How frequently to update the `max-event-age` pruning progress metric in the background. A setting of None disables background metric updating. |
| `pruning.traffic-purchased-retention` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `1 hour` | Retention duration on how long to retain traffic purchased entry updates for each member |
| `reader.checkpoint-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | how frequently to checkpoint state |
| `reader.event-generation-parallelism` | int | `4` | how many events will be generated from the fetched payloads in parallel |
| `reader.payload-batch-size` | int | `10` | max number of payloads to fetch from the datastore in one page |
| `reader.payload-batch-window` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 milliseconds` | max time window to wait for more payloads before fetching the current batch from the datastore |
| `reader.payload-fetch-parallelism` | int | `2` | how many batches of payloads will be fetched in parallel |
| `reader.polling-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | _unset_ | how frequently to poll for new events from the database. only used in the enterprise edition if high availability has been configured, otherwise will rely on local writes performed by this sequencer to indicate that new events are available. |
| `reader.read-batch-size` | int | `100` | max number of events to fetch from the datastore in one page |
| `testing-interceptor` | string (TestingInterceptor) | _unset_ |  |
| `writer.type` | one of `low-latency`, `high-throughput` | **required** | Configuration for the database based sequencer writer |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

Further types inside this one: [SequencerWriterConfig](#sequencerwriterconfig) at `writer.type`.

#### `type = external`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer {
  type = external
  block {
    circuit-breaker {
      enabled = true
      messages {
        acknowledgement {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        commitment {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1

          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"

        }

          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1

        }

          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        top-up {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        topology {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        unexpected {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        verdict {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"

        }
      }
    }
    reader {
      checkpoint-interval = "5 seconds"
      event-generation-parallelism = 4

      messages {
        confirmation-request {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }
        confirmation-response {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }

          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }
      }
      observation-period-seconds = 60
      strict = true
      thresholds = [ ]
      update-every-ms = 100
    }
    writer {
      type = low-latency|high-throughput   # required
    }
  }
  config = "..."   # required
  sequencer-type = "..."   # required
}
```

#### `type = bft-sequencer`
