<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer {
  type = bft-sequencer
  block {
    circuit-breaker {
      enabled = true
      messages {
        acknowledgement {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        commitment {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        confirmation-request {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        confirmation-response {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        lsu-sequencing-test {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        time-proof {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        top-up {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        topology {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        unexpected {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        verdict {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
      }
    }
    reader {
      checkpoint-interval = "5 seconds"
      event-generation-parallelism = 4
      payload-batch-size = 10
      payload-batch-window = "5 milliseconds"
      payload-fetch-parallelism = 2
      # polling-interval = <duration>
      read-batch-size = 100
    }
    stream-instrumentation {
      buffer-size = 256
      is-enabled = false
    }
    # testing-interceptor = <string>
    throughput-cap {
      clock-tick-interval = "100 milliseconds"
      delayed-activation = true
      enabled = false
      messages {
        confirmation-request {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }
        confirmation-response {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }
        topology {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }
      }
      observation-period-seconds = 60
      strict = true
      thresholds = [ ]
      update-every-ms = 100
    }
    writer {
      type = low-latency|high-throughput   # required
    }
  }
  config {
    availability-dissemination-patience = "5 seconds"
    availability-max-non-ordered-batches-per-node = 1000
    availability-min-proposal-creation-delay = "250 milliseconds"
    availability-number-of-attempts-of-downloading-output-fetch-before-warning = 5
    batch-aggregator {
      type = batching|no-batching   # required
    }
    blocking-db-read-timeout = "1 minute"
    consensus-block-completion-timeout = "10 seconds"
    consensus-empty-block-creation-timeout = "500 milliseconds"
    consensus-enable-flushing-segment = true
    consensus-enable-log-end-of-epoch-progress = false
    consensus-flushing-min-blocks = 2
    consensus-new-epoch-topology-warn-timeout = "2 seconds"
    # consensus-queue-max-size = <int>
    consensus-queue-per-node-quota = 1024
    # dedicated-execution-context-divisor = <int>
    delayed-init-queue-max-size = 1024
    enable-performance-metrics = true
    # epoch-state-transfer-future-epoch-queue-max-size = <int>
    epoch-state-transfer-future-epoch-queue-per-node-quota = 1024
    epoch-state-transfer-how-many-future-epochs-to-download-in-parallel = 5
    epoch-state-transfer-retry-timeout = "4 seconds"
    epoch-state-transfer-timeout-for-future-epoch = "500 milliseconds"
    # init-query-timeout = <duration>
    # init-timeout = <duration>
    initial-network {
      connection-management-config {
        # connection-retry-delay-multiplier = <int>
        flow-control-buffer = 128
        flow-control-buffer-drop-newest = false
        # flow-control-ready-allowance = <int>
        initial-connection-max-delay = "500 milliseconds"
        initial-connection-retry-delay = "500 milliseconds"
        max-connection-attempts-before-warning = 30
        max-connection-retry-delay = "2 minutes"
      }
      endpoint-authentication {
        auth-token {
          backoff {
            base = 2
            jitter {
              type = equal|full   # required
            }
            max-retry-interval = "2 minutes"
          }
          min-retry-interval = "500 milliseconds"
          refresh-auth-token-before-expiry = "20 seconds"
          retries = 20
        }
        enabled = true
      }
      overwrite-stored-endpoints = false
      peer-endpoints = [
        {
          address = "..."   # required
          channel {
            # flow-control-window = <int>
            # initial-flow-control-window = <int>
            keep-alive-client {
              idle-timeout = "30 minutes"
              keep-alive-without-calls = false
              time = "40 seconds"
              timeout = "15 seconds"
            }
            max-inbound-message-size = 0   # required
            trace-context-propagation = enabled|disabled   # required
          }
          port = 0   # required
          tls-config {
            client-cert {
              cert-chain-file {
                type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
              }
              private-key-file = "..."   # required
            }
            enabled = true
            trust-collection-file {
              type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
            }
          }
        }
      ]
      server-endpoint {
        address = "..."   # required
        external-address = "..."   # required
        external-port = 0   # required
        external-tls-config {
          client-cert {
            cert-chain-file {
              type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
            }
            private-key-file = "..."   # required
          }
          enabled = true
          trust-collection-file {
            type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
          }
        }
        # flow-control-window = <int>
        # initial-flow-control-window = <int>
        keep-alive-server {
          permit-keep-alive-time = "20 seconds"
          permit-keep-alive-without-calls = false
          time = "40 seconds"
          timeout = "20 seconds"
        }
        limits {
          active {
            <key> = [ ]
          }
          throttle-logging-rate-per-second = 10
          warn-on-undefined-limits = false
        }
        max-concurrent-calls-per-connection = 100000
        # max-inbound-message-size = <int>
        # port = <int>
        tls {
          cert-chain-file {
            type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
          }
          # ciphers = [ ... ]
          client-auth {
            type = require|optional|none   # required
          }
          enable-cert-revocation-checking = false
          minimum-server-protocol-version = "TLSv1.2"
          private-key-file = "..."   # required
          trust-collection-file {
            type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
          }
        }
      }
    }
    leader-selection-policy-config-for-pv-34 {
      how-long-to-blacklist {
        type = linear|linear-with-parameters|exponential|no-blacklisting   # required
      }
      how-many-can-we-blacklist = num-faults-tolerated|no-blacklisting   # required
    }
    manual-vacuum-enabled = false
    max-batch-creation-interval = "100 milliseconds"
    # max-mempool-queue-size = <int>
    min-requests-in-batch = 3
    network-send-attempts = 5
    # network-send-retry-jitter-cap = <duration>
    # network-send-retry-minimum-delay = <duration>
    # output-enqueue-max-retries = <int>
    output-enqueue-max-retry-delay = "5 seconds"
    output-fetch-how-many-recipients = 2
    output-fetch-minimum-delay = "200 milliseconds"
    output-fetch-timeout = "200 milliseconds"
    output-fetch-timeout-cap = "200 milliseconds"
    output-size-of-chunk-of-epochs-to-load-at-start = 10
    # segment-length-for-pv-34 = <int>
    send-blacklist-ttl = "3 minutes"
    sequencer-core-subscription-config {
      pause-orderer-threshold-buffer-size = 5000
      pekko-queue-source-buffer-size = 5000
      resume-orderer-threshold-buffer-size = 1000
    }
    standalone {
      blacklist-leader-selection-policy-config {
        how-long-to-blacklist {
          type = linear|linear-with-parameters|exponential|no-blacklisting   # required
        }
        how-many-can-we-blacklist = num-faults-tolerated|no-blacklisting   # required
      }
      max-batches-per-block-proposal = 0   # required
      max-requests-in-batch = 0   # required
      pbft-view-change-timeout = "30 seconds"   # required
      peers = [
        {
          sequencer-id = "..."   # required
          signing-public-key-proto-file = "/path/to/file"   # required
        }
      ]
      segment-length = 0   # required
      signing-private-key-proto-file = "/path/to/file"   # required
      signing-public-key-proto-file = "/path/to/file"   # required
      test-slowdown {
        post-ordering-delay {
          type = constant-distribution|weighted-distribution|linear-distribution|power-distribution   # required
        }
        send-delay {
          default-delay-distribution {
            type = constant-distribution|weighted-distribution|linear-distribution|power-distribution   # required
          }
          delays-by-recipients = [
            {
              delay-distribution {
                type = constant-distribution|weighted-distribution|linear-distribution|power-distribution   # required
              }
              instance-names = [ ]   # required
              # probability-of-grpc-send-accepted-by-flow-control = <number>
              # probability-of-grpc-send-success = <number>
            }
          ]
        }
        topology-delay {
          # broadcast-request-probability = <number>
          get-ordering-topology-delay {
            type = constant-distribution|weighted-distribution|linear-distribution|power-distribution   # required
          }
          # pending-topology-changes-probability = <number>
          # possible-ordering-topology-change-in-request-probability = <number>
          request-inspection-delay {
            type = constant-distribution|weighted-distribution|linear-distribution|power-distribution   # required
          }
        }
      }
      this-sequencer-id = "..."   # required
    }
    storage {
      type = memory|h2|postgres   # required
    }
    # view-change-timeout-override = <duration>
  }
  # config.initial-network.server-endpoint.tls.client-auth.admin-client.cert-chain-file.type takes a type of its own, documented separately
}
```

| `block.reader.checkpoint-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | how frequently to checkpoint state |
| `block.reader.event-generation-parallelism` | int | `4` | how many events will be generated from the fetched payloads in parallel |
| `block.reader.payload-batch-size` | int | `10` | max number of payloads to fetch from the datastore in one page |
| `block.reader.payload-batch-window` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 milliseconds` | max time window to wait for more payloads before fetching the current batch from the datastore |
| `block.reader.payload-fetch-parallelism` | int | `2` | how many batches of payloads will be fetched in parallel |
| `block.reader.polling-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | _unset_ | how frequently to poll for new events from the database. only used in the enterprise edition if high availability has been configured, otherwise will rely on local writes performed by this sequencer to indicate that new events are available. |
| `block.reader.read-batch-size` | int | `100` | max number of events to fetch from the datastore in one page |
| `block.stream-instrumentation.buffer-size` | int (PositiveInt) | `256` (in practice `128`) ◊ |  |
| `block.stream-instrumentation.is-enabled` | boolean | `false` |  |
| `block.testing-interceptor` | string (TestingInterceptor) | _unset_ |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.clock-tick-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `100 milliseconds` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.delayed-activation` | boolean | `true` | If true (default) then the limits will be enforced after an initial grace period equal to the observationPeriodSeconds. |
| `block.throughput-cap.enabled` | boolean | `false` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.confirmation-request.global-kbps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `2000` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.confirmation-request.global-tps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `10` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.confirmation-request.per-client-kbps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `1000` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.confirmation-request.per-client-tps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `4` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.confirmation-response.global-kbps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `2000` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.confirmation-response.global-tps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `10` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.confirmation-response.per-client-kbps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `1000` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.confirmation-response.per-client-tps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `4` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.topology.global-kbps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `2000` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.topology.global-tps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `10` (in practice `2`) ◊ |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.topology.per-client-kbps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `1000` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.messages.topology.per-client-tps-cap` | number (PositiveDouble) | `4` (in practice `1`) ◊ |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.observation-period-seconds` | int | `60` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.strict` | boolean | `true` | how should the per member rate cap be computed |
| `block.throughput-cap.thresholds` | array of PositiveDouble | `0.9` |  |
| `block.throughput-cap.update-every-ms` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `100` | how often should the new caps be computed (only used in non-strict mode) |
| `block.writer.type` | one of `low-latency`, `high-throughput` | `high-throughput` † | Configuration for the database based sequencer writer |
| `config.availability-dissemination-patience` | duration | `5 seconds` | The amount of time that the availability module waits for a dissemination acknowledgement from a peer before re-sending the batch. If `None`, re-sending is disabled. |
| `config.availability-max-non-ordered-batches-per-node` | int | `1000` | The maximum number of unordered batches that the availability module will accept from each peer. The purpose of this limit is to prevent malicious peers from consuming memory and storage by flooding new batches to correct nodes. |
| `config.availability-min-proposal-creation-delay` | duration | `250 milliseconds` | The minimum delay between consecutive proposal creations in the availability module. This prevents the node from creating proposals too frequently. |
| `config.availability-number-of-attempts-of-downloading-output-fetch-before-warning` | int | `5` | The number of consecutive failed attempts to retrieve data for an ordered batch before the ordering node emits a warning to the logs |
| `config.batch-aggregator.type` | one of `batching`, `no-batching` | **required** | Parameters for that batcher that batches queries (e.g., to a DB). |
| `config.blocking-db-read-timeout` | duration | `1 minute` | The maximum time allowed for block reads to restore state from the database after restarts. Currently, if the timeout is reached during any of the blocking reads on startup, the node shuts down and must restart to try again. |
| `config.consensus-block-completion-timeout` | duration | `10 seconds` | The maximum time that nodes will wait for each leader to order the next expected block in the corresponding index. After each block in a segment is ordered (and at the start of each epoch), this timeout is reset for that corresponding segment. If the timeout is reached for a given segment, the node will vote for a View Change. |
| `config.consensus-empty-block-creation-timeout` | duration | `500 milliseconds` | The time after which a leader will create an empty block if there is no regular block available to propose, in order to ensure that: (i) View Changes are avoided, and (ii) BFT time advances at a regular frequency. Note that due to (i), it is recommended that this consensusEmptyBlockCreationTimeout be substantially less than the consensusBlockCompletionTimeout to account for latency, retransmissions, etc. |
| `config.consensus-enable-flushing-segment` | boolean | `true` | If `true`, when this node detects that a strong quorum of segments are completed while the one it leads is in progress, it will flush the segment and complete all slots in parallel to avoid making other nodes wait for the epoch to complete. This is a performance optimization that can be disabled if issues arise with the flushing logic. |
| `config.consensus-enable-log-end-of-epoch-progress` | boolean | `false` | If `true`, logs are emitted to track progress at the end of each epoch with the status of segments from all leaders. |
| `config.consensus-flushing-min-blocks` | int | `2` | The minimum number of blocks that must be missing to complete in a segment before the node will be able to decide to flush the segment and complete all slots in parallel if it detects that a strong quorum of segments are completed while the one it leads is in progress. If fewer blocks than this are missing, the node will complete them normally one at a time. |
| `config.consensus-new-epoch-topology-warn-timeout` | duration | `2 seconds` | The time that nodes will wait at the start of a new epoch for the topology to be activated. If the timeout is reached, the node will create a warning log to indicate the reason for delay in progress was the topology activation, but the node will continue to wait for the topology to be activated. |
| `config.consensus-queue-max-size` | int | `DefaultConsensusQueueMaxSize` ‡ | The maximum size per consensus-related queue. |
| `config.consensus-queue-per-node-quota` | int | `1024` | The maximum number of messages per node stored in consensus-related queues (quotas are maintained separately per queue). |
| `config.dedicated-execution-context-divisor` | int | `DefaultDedicatedExecutionContextDivisor` ‡ | Optionally, a dedicated execution context for the BFT orderer. When set, the BFT orderer creates an independent execution context, separate from the colocated sequencer, using `Threading.detectNumberOfThreads` / `dedicatedExecutionContextDivisor`. This is potentially useful in deployments with heavy execution context contention between the sequencer and BFT ordering layer. If set to the default None, the BFT orderer shares the same execution context as the sequencer. |
| `config.delayed-init-queue-max-size` | int | `1024` | The maximum size of the delayed init queue. This queue is used by modules to save incoming events in memory while the module is still initializing. Once startup is complete, the module processes all events from the delayed init queue first before continuing to read newly received events. |
| `config.enable-performance-metrics` | boolean | `true` | If `true`, the BFT layer emits metrics to track real-time and historical performance. |
| `config.epoch-state-transfer-future-epoch-queue-max-size` | int | `DefaultConsensusQueueMaxSize` ‡ | The maximum size of future epoch queue. |
| `config.epoch-state-transfer-future-epoch-queue-per-node-quota` | int | `1024` | The maximum number of messages per node stored in future epoch queue |
| `config.epoch-state-transfer-how-many-future-epochs-to-download-in-parallel` | int (NonNegativeLong) | `5` | The amount of epochs to speculatively download in parallel during State Transfer. Can be 0 to turn off speculative downloading |
| `config.epoch-state-transfer-retry-timeout` | duration | `4 seconds` | The state transfer retry timeout covering periods from requesting blocks from a single epoch up to receiving all the corresponding batches. |
| `config.epoch-state-transfer-timeout-for-future-epoch` | duration | `500 milliseconds` | A timeout for how long we will wait if we already potentially have all blocks due to speculatively downloaded an epoch before we make a new network request. This timeout should be quite short since it is only accounting for local processing. |
| `config.init-query-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `DefaultInitQueryTimeout` ‡ | The maximum time allowed for individual queries during BFT block orderer initialization (e.g., topology or state queries). Must be less than or equal to `initTimeout`. |
| `config.init-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `DefaultInitTimeout` ‡ | The maximum total time allowed for the BFT block orderer to complete initialization, including all startup queries and state restoration. If exceeded, the node fails to start. |
| `config.initial-network.connection-management-config.connection-retry-delay-multiplier` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `NonNegativeInt.two` ‡ | The backoff factor applied to the delay between subsequent failed retry attempts |
| `config.initial-network.connection-management-config.flow-control-buffer` | int (PositiveInt) | `128` | If set, enables P2P flow control on the sender side with the specified buffer size. If not set, the gRPC implementation will manage send buffers in case of slow receivers (and may OOM). |
| `config.initial-network.connection-management-config.flow-control-buffer-drop-newest` | boolean | `false` | If true, newest excess sends, rather than oldest, will be dropped from the flow control buffer. |
| `config.initial-network.connection-management-config.flow-control-ready-allowance` | int (PositiveInt) | `PositiveInt.one` ‡ | If `flowControlBuffer` is set, a "ready to send" signal will be valid for the specified number of messages without further checks. |
| `config.initial-network.connection-management-config.initial-connection-max-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `500 milliseconds` | The base max delay between retry attempts, used to bootstrap the jitter mechanism |
| `config.initial-network.connection-management-config.initial-connection-retry-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `500 milliseconds` | The base delay between retry attempts when connecting to a peer |
| `config.initial-network.connection-management-config.max-connection-attempts-before-warning` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `30` | The number of failed connection attempts after which a WARN log is emitted |
| `config.initial-network.connection-management-config.max-connection-retry-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `2 minutes` | The maximum delay between retry attempts to connect to a peer |
| `config.initial-network.endpoint-authentication.auth-token.backoff.base` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `2` |  |
| `config.initial-network.endpoint-authentication.auth-token.backoff.jitter.type` | one of `equal`, `full` | **required** |  |
| `config.initial-network.endpoint-authentication.auth-token.backoff.max-retry-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `2 minutes` |  |
| `config.initial-network.endpoint-authentication.auth-token.min-retry-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `500 milliseconds` |  |
| `config.initial-network.endpoint-authentication.auth-token.refresh-auth-token-before-expiry` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `20 seconds` | how much time before the auth token expires should we fetch a new one? |
| `config.initial-network.endpoint-authentication.auth-token.retries` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `20` |  |
| `config.initial-network.endpoint-authentication.enabled` | boolean | `true` | Whether authentication should be setup between peers. For security reasons, this should NOT be disabled in any real production deployments, unless a decision is explicitly made to exclusively rely on mTLS for peer-to-peer authentication. |
| `config.initial-network.overwrite-stored-endpoints` | boolean | `false` | whether the peers in storage should be replaced by this config's peers endpoint on startup |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints` | array of P2PEndpointConfig | `[]` | set of peers that the local node expects to connect to on startup |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].address` | string | **required** |  |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].channel.flow-control-window` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ | Switches to manual gRPC flow control and sets its window; if `None`, then it is not configured and the implementation default is used. At most one of `flowControlWindow` and `initialFlowControlWindow` can be set. |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].channel.initial-flow-control-window` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ | Switches to automatic gRPC flow control and sets its initial window; if `None`, then it is not configured and the implementation default is used. At most one of `flowControlWindow` and `initialFlowControlWindow` can be set. |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].channel.keep-alive-client.idle-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `30 minutes` | If there are no RPCs during this duration, the channel will close all connections and switch to Idle state (default is 30 min) |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].channel.keep-alive-client.keep-alive-without-calls` | boolean | `false` | Enables sending of keep alive even when there are no RPCs on the channel (default to false). This increases network resource consumption for idle connections and requires the server to enable permitKeepAliveWithoutCalls. Prefer using idleTimeout. |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].channel.keep-alive-client.time` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `40 seconds` | Sets the time without read activity before sending a keepalive ping. Do not set to small numbers (default is 40s) |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].channel.keep-alive-client.timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `15 seconds` | Sets the time waiting for read activity after sending a keepalive ping (default is 15s). The default timeout was previously 20s and was lowered to 15s, because besides configuring the gRPC KeepAliveManager, it also sets up the socket's TCP_USER_TIMEOUT (see html). 15s gives a larger margin to detect a faulty connection earlier and retry a submission on another sequencer via amplification, thereby avoiding a request failure. |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].channel.max-inbound-message-size` | int (NonNegativeInt) | **required** |  |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].channel.trace-context-propagation` | one of `enabled`, `disabled` | **required** |  |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].port` | int (Port) | **required** |  |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].tls-config.client-cert.cert-chain-file.type` | one of `pem-file`, `pem-string` | **required** | A class representing either an existing PEM file path or an inlined PEM string for configuration file fields |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].tls-config.client-cert.private-key-file` | string (PemFile) | **required** |  |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].tls-config.enabled` | boolean | `true` | allows enabling TLS without `trustCollectionFile` or `clientCert` |
| `config.initial-network.peer-endpoints[].tls-config.trust-collection-file.type` | one of `pem-file`, `pem-string` | **required** | A class representing either an existing PEM file path or an inlined PEM string for configuration file fields |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.address` | string | **required** | The internal binding address for the server. |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.external-address` | string | **required** | The external address that remote peers should connect to in order to reach the local server. In practice, this may not be the actual internal address that the local server listens on, and instead may represent, for example, an intermediate TLS-terminating proxy. |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.external-port` | int (Port) | **required** | The external port that remote peers should connect to in order to reach the local server. In practice, this may not be the actual internal internalPort that the local server listens on, and instead may represent, for example, an intermediate TLS-terminating proxy. |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.external-tls-config.client-cert.cert-chain-file.type` | one of `pem-file`, `pem-string` | **required** | A class representing either an existing PEM file path or an inlined PEM string for configuration file fields |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.external-tls-config.client-cert.private-key-file` | string (PemFile) | **required** |  |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.external-tls-config.enabled` | boolean | `true` | allows enabling TLS without `trustCollectionFile` or `clientCert` |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.external-tls-config.trust-collection-file.type` | one of `pem-file`, `pem-string` | **required** | A class representing either an existing PEM file path or an inlined PEM string for configuration file fields |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.flow-control-window` | int (PositiveInt) | `ServerConfig.defaultFlowControlWindow` ‡ |  |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.initial-flow-control-window` | int (PositiveInt) | `ServerConfig.defaultInitialFlowControlWindow` ‡ |  |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.keep-alive-server.permit-keep-alive-time` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `20 seconds` |  |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.keep-alive-server.permit-keep-alive-without-calls` | boolean | `false` |  |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.keep-alive-server.time` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `40 seconds` |  |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.keep-alive-server.timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `20 seconds` |  |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.limits.active.<key>` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `[]` | map of service name to maximum number of parallel active requests or streams |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.limits.throttle-logging-rate-per-second` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `10` | maximum rate for logging rejections for requests exceeding the limit (prevents DOS on logs) |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.limits.warn-on-undefined-limits` | boolean | `false` | emit warning if a limit is not configured for a stream |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.max-concurrent-calls-per-connection` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `100000` |  |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.max-inbound-message-size` | int (NonNegativeInt) | `ServerConfig.defaultMaxInboundMessageSize` ‡ | maximum allowed size of incoming messages |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.port` | int (Port) | _unset_ | The internal binding port for the server. |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.tls.cert-chain-file.type` | one of `pem-file`, `pem-string` | **required** | A class representing either an existing PEM file path or an inlined PEM string for configuration file fields |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.tls.ciphers` | array of string | `TlsConfig.defaultCiphers` ‡ | supported ciphers. Set to None (or null in config file) to use default settings defaultCiphers |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.tls.client-auth.type` | one of `require`, `optional`, `none` | **required** | Configuration on whether server requires auth, requests auth, or no auth |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.tls.enable-cert-revocation-checking` | boolean | `false` | whether to enable certificate revocation checking per https://tersesystems.com/blog/2014/03/22/fixing-certificate-revocation/ |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.tls.minimum-server-protocol-version` | string | `TLSv1.2` | minimum supported TLS protocol. Set None (or null in config file) to use default settings defaultMinimumServerProtocol |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.tls.private-key-file` | string (PemFile) | **required** | a file containing the server's private key. The key must not use a password. |
| `config.initial-network.server-endpoint.tls.trust-collection-file.type` | one of `pem-file`, `pem-string` | **required** | A class representing either an existing PEM file path or an inlined PEM string for configuration file fields |
| `config.leader-selection-policy-config-for-pv-34.how-long-to-blacklist.type` | one of `linear`, `linear-with-parameters`, `exponential`, `no-blacklisting` | **required** |  |
| `config.leader-selection-policy-config-for-pv-34.how-many-can-we-blacklist` | one of `num-faults-tolerated`, `no-blacklisting` | **required** | The number of peers that can be simultaneously blacklisted, and defaults to the number of tolerated faults: f = (N-1)/3. |
| `config.manual-vacuum-enabled` | boolean | `false` | If `true`, the BFT block orderer will attempt to manually vacuum each partition after it becomes cold. |
| `config.max-batch-creation-interval` | duration | `100 milliseconds` | The maximum amount of time the mempool will wait for incoming requests before creating and sending a batch to the availability module. |
| `config.max-mempool-queue-size` | int | `DefaultMaxMempoolQueueSize` ‡ | The maximum number of pending requests that will be held in the in-memory mempool. Once this queue size is reached, subsequent requests are rejected with a mempool overloaded error. |
| `config.min-requests-in-batch` | int | `3` | When the mempool does not create and send a batch due to other factors (see maxBatchCreationInterval), this is the number of requests that the mempool will wait for before shipping a batch to the availability module. |
| `config.network-send-attempts` | int (PositiveInt) | `5` | The maximum number of gRPC message send attempts before dropping the message. |
| `config.network-send-retry-jitter-cap` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `DefaultNetworkSendRetryMaximumDelay` ‡ | The maximum jittered delay that a node will use to wait between consecutive gRPC message send attempts. The minimum delay is added to the jittered delay. |
| `config.network-send-retry-minimum-delay` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `DefaultNetworkSendRetryMinimumDelay` ‡ | The minimum delay between consecutive gRPC message send attempts. This prevents a node from overwhelming peers with rapid successive gRPC message sends, for example if the jitter returns very low values. |
| `config.output-enqueue-max-retries` | int | `DefaultOutputEnqueueMaxRetries` ‡ | The maximum number of retry attempts when enqueuing ordered output blocks for delivery to the sequencer core. |
| `config.output-enqueue-max-retry-delay` | duration | `5 seconds` | The maximum delay between retry attempts when enqueuing ordered output blocks for delivery to the sequencer core. |
| `config.output-fetch-how-many-recipients` | int (PositiveInt) | `2` | The number of recipients that a batch will be attempted to be fetched from in parallel. |
| `config.output-fetch-minimum-delay` | duration | `200 milliseconds` | The minimum delay between consecutive output fetch attempts. This prevents a node from overwhelming peers with rapid successive fetch requests, for example if the jitter returns very low values. |
| `config.output-fetch-timeout` | duration | `200 milliseconds` | The baseline timeout that a node's availability module will wait while fetching batch data from a peer before giving up and trying a different peer. Timeouts for subsequent fetch attempts are increased via exponential backoff and random jitter between 0 and the computed value. The unit is important, as the exponential is computed on the non-converted value. |
| `config.output-fetch-timeout-cap` | duration | `200 milliseconds` | The maximum jittered duration that a node's availability module will wait while fetching batch data from a peer. The minimum delay is added to the jittered delay. |
| `config.output-size-of-chunk-of-epochs-to-load-at-start` | int | `10` | The number of epochs to load per chunk when restoring output state from the database on startup. Larger values may speed up startup but consume more memory. |
| `config.segment-length-for-pv-34` | int | _unset_ | Optionally, the number of blocks per segment (only taken into account in Protocol Version 34). If set to None, the default epoch length is used. |
| `config.send-blacklist-ttl` | duration | `3 minutes` | The time-to-live for blacklisting randomly chosen peers that have failed to respond to a request as part of a workflow (e.g., batches fetching or a state transfer session). After this time has elapsed, the peer is removed from the blacklist and can be re-contacted. |
| `config.sequencer-core-subscription-config.pause-orderer-threshold-buffer-size` | int | `5000` | The threshold buffer size after which the sequencer core signals the orderer to pause. |
| `config.sequencer-core-subscription-config.pekko-queue-source-buffer-size` | int | `5000` | The buffer size of the Pekko source queue that provides blocks from the orderer to the sequencer core. |
| `config.sequencer-core-subscription-config.resume-orderer-threshold-buffer-size` | int | `1000` | The threshold buffer size at or below which the sequencer core signals the orderer to resume after a pause. |
| `config.standalone.blacklist-leader-selection-policy-config.how-long-to-blacklist.type` | one of `linear`, `linear-with-parameters`, `exponential`, `no-blacklisting` | **required** |  |
| `config.standalone.blacklist-leader-selection-policy-config.how-many-can-we-blacklist` | one of `num-faults-tolerated`, `no-blacklisting` | **required** | The number of peers that can be simultaneously blacklisted, and defaults to the number of tolerated faults: f = (N-1)/3. |
| `config.standalone.max-batches-per-block-proposal` | int | **required** | The maximum number of batches that can be included in a single block proposal. |
| `config.standalone.max-requests-in-batch` | int | **required** | The maximum number of requests that can be included in a single batch. |
| `config.standalone.pbft-view-change-timeout` | duration | **required** | The base duration to use for view change timeouts for ISS segments. |
| `config.standalone.peers` | array of BftBlockOrderingStandalonePeerConfig | **required** | The list of peers in the standalone network, including their sequencer IDs and public keys. |
| `config.standalone.peers[].sequencer-id` | string | **required** | The sequencer ID of the peer. |
| `config.standalone.peers[].signing-public-key-proto-file` | path | **required** | The file containing the public key used to verify incoming messages from the peer. |
| `config.standalone.segment-length` | int | **required** | The number of blocks per segment. |
| `config.standalone.signing-private-key-proto-file` | path | **required** | The file containing the private key used to sign outgoing messages. |
| `config.standalone.signing-public-key-proto-file` | path | **required** | The file containing the public key used to verify incoming messages. |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.post-ordering-delay.type` | one of `constant-distribution`, `weighted-distribution`, `linear-distribution`, `power-distribution` | **required** |  |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.send-delay.default-delay-distribution.type` | one of `constant-distribution`, `weighted-distribution`, `linear-distribution`, `power-distribution` | **required** |  |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.send-delay.delays-by-recipients` | array of DelayByRecipients | `[]` | Optional list of specific delay distributions and failure probabilities applied to specific recipients. |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.send-delay.delays-by-recipients[].delay-distribution.type` | one of `constant-distribution`, `weighted-distribution`, `linear-distribution`, `power-distribution` | **required** |  |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.send-delay.delays-by-recipients[].instance-names` | array of string | **required** |  |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.send-delay.delays-by-recipients[].probability-of-grpc-send-accepted-by-flow-control` | number | _unset_ |  |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.send-delay.delays-by-recipients[].probability-of-grpc-send-success` | number | _unset_ |  |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.topology-delay.broadcast-request-probability` | number | _unset_ | Optional probability of a submission request being a broadcast, which triggers a deeper and more costly inspection about whether it may affect the ordering topology. |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.topology-delay.get-ordering-topology-delay.type` | one of `constant-distribution`, `weighted-distribution`, `linear-distribution`, `power-distribution` | **required** |  |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.topology-delay.pending-topology-changes-probability` | number | _unset_ | Optional probability of the ordering topology returned by `getOrderingTopology` signalling that there are pending topology changes, which triggers getting an up-to-date ordering topology again before starting the subsequent epoch, regardless of whether broadcast submission requests are going to be ordered before the end of the epoch. |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.topology-delay.possible-ordering-topology-change-in-request-probability` | number | _unset_ | Optional probability of detecting that a broadcast submission request may also alter the ordering topology for the subsequent epoch, which triggers getting an up-to-date ordering topology via `getOrderingTopology` before starting a new epoch. |
| `config.standalone.test-slowdown.topology-delay.request-inspection-delay.type` | one of `constant-distribution`, `weighted-distribution`, `linear-distribution`, `power-distribution` | **required** |  |
| `config.standalone.this-sequencer-id` | string | **required** | The sequencer ID of the local node. |
| `config.storage.type` | one of `memory`, `h2`, `postgres` | `memory` † | Determines how a node stores persistent data. |
| `config.view-change-timeout-override` | duration | _unset_ | Optionally, the base duration to use for view change timeouts for ISS segments. If specified, this value will be used instead of the pbftViewChangeTimeout set in the network-wide SequencingParameters topology transaction. Note that this value is not persisted (in-memory only), and must be set back to None to return using the SequencingParameters value. |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### SequencerWriterConfig

Set `type` to one of `low-latency`, `high-throughput`.

Occurs at:

- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.block.writer`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.writer`

#### `type = low-latency`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.writer {
  type = low-latency
  # buffered-events-max-memory = <int>
  buffered-events-preload-batch-size = 50
  # checkpoint-backfill-parallelism = <int>
  checkpoint-interval = "30 seconds"
  # commit-mode-validation = synchronous|local
  event-write-batch-max-size = 1
  event-write-max-concurrency = 1
  payload-queue-size = 1000
  payload-to-event-margin = "60 seconds"
  payload-write-batch-max-size = 1
  payload-write-max-concurrency = 2
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `buffered-events-max-memory` | int (BytesUnit) | `DefaultBufferedEventsMaxMemory` ‡ |  |
| `buffered-events-preload-batch-size` | int (PositiveInt) | `50` |  |
| `checkpoint-backfill-parallelism` | int (PositiveInt) | `DefaultCheckpointBackfillParallelism` ‡ |  |
| `checkpoint-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `30 seconds` |  |
| `commit-mode-validation` | one of `synchronous`, `local` | `CommitMode.Default.some` ‡ |  |
| `event-write-batch-max-size` | int | `1` |  |
| `event-write-max-concurrency` | int | `1` |  |
| `payload-queue-size` | int | `1000` |  |
| `payload-to-event-margin` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `60 seconds` |  |
| `payload-write-batch-max-size` | int | `1` |  |
| `payload-write-max-concurrency` | int | `2` |  |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

#### `type = high-throughput`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.writer {
  type = high-throughput
  # buffered-events-max-memory = <int>
  buffered-events-preload-batch-size = 50
  # checkpoint-backfill-parallelism = <int>
  checkpoint-interval = "30 seconds"
  # commit-mode-validation = synchronous|local
  event-write-batch-max-size = 100
  event-write-max-concurrency = 1
  payload-queue-size = 1000
  payload-to-event-margin = "60 seconds"
  payload-write-batch-max-size = 50
  payload-write-max-concurrency = 4
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `buffered-events-max-memory` | int (BytesUnit) | `DefaultBufferedEventsMaxMemory` ‡ |  |
| `buffered-events-preload-batch-size` | int (PositiveInt) | `50` |  |
| `checkpoint-backfill-parallelism` | int (PositiveInt) | `DefaultCheckpointBackfillParallelism` ‡ |  |
| `checkpoint-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `30 seconds` |  |
| `commit-mode-validation` | one of `synchronous`, `local` | `CommitMode.Default.some` ‡ |  |
| `event-write-batch-max-size` | int | `100` |  |
| `event-write-max-concurrency` | int | `1` |  |
| `payload-queue-size` | int | `1000` |  |
| `payload-to-event-margin` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `60 seconds` |  |
| `payload-write-batch-max-size` | int | `50` |  |
| `payload-write-max-concurrency` | int | `4` |  |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### ServerAuthRequirementConfig

Set `type` to one of `require`, `optional`, `none`.

Occurs at:

- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api.tls.client-auth`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.config.initial-network.server-endpoint.tls.client-auth`

#### `type = require`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api.tls.client-auth {
  type = require
  admin-client {
    cert-chain-file {
      type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
    }
    private-key-file = "..."   # required
  }
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `admin-client.cert-chain-file.type` | one of `pem-file`, `pem-string` | **required** | A class representing either an existing PEM file path or an inlined PEM string for configuration file fields |
| `admin-client.private-key-file` | string (PemFile) | **required** |  |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

Further types inside this one: [PemFileOrString](#pemfileorstring) at `admin-client.cert-chain-file.type`.

#### `type = optional`

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api.tls.client-auth {
  type = optional
  # takes no further keys
}
```

#### `type = none`

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api.tls.client-auth {
  type = none
  # takes no further keys
}
```

### StorageConfig

Set `type` to one of `memory`, `h2`, `postgres`.

Occurs at:

- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer.config.storage`
- `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.storage`

#### `type = memory`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.storage {
  type = memory
  parameters {
    connection-allocation {
      # num-ledger-api = <int>
      # num-reads = <int>
      # num-writes = <int>
    }
    connection-timeout = "5 seconds"
    fail-fast-on-startup = true
    failed-to-fatal-delay = "5 minutes"
    # max-connections = <int>
    migrate-and-start = false
    migrations-paths = [ ]
    partitions {
      initial-bft-orderer-tables-partition-size = 1500
    }
    repeatable-migrations-paths = [ ]
    unsafe-baseline-on-migrate = false
    unsafe-clean-on-validation-error = false
    # warn-on-slow-query = <duration>
    warn-on-slow-query-interval = "5 seconds"
  }
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `parameters.connection-allocation.num-ledger-api` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ |  |
| `parameters.connection-allocation.num-reads` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ |  |
| `parameters.connection-allocation.num-writes` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ |  |
| `parameters.connection-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` (in practice `30 seconds`) ◊ | How long to wait for acquiring a database connection |
| `parameters.fail-fast-on-startup` | boolean | `true` | If true, the node will fail-fast when the database cannot be connected to If false, the node will wait indefinitely for the database to come up |
| `parameters.failed-to-fatal-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | `5 minutes` | Delay after which, if the storage is continuously in a Failed state, it will escalate to Fatal. The default value is 5 minutes. Components that use the storage as a health dependency can then determine how to react. Currently, the sequencer declares it as a fatal dependency for its liveness health, which means it will transition to NOT_SERVING if this delay is exceeded, allowing a monitoring infrastructure to restart it. **NOTE**: Currently this only applies to DbStorageSingle, which is only used by the sequencer. TODO(i24240): Apply the same behavior to `DbStorageMulti` |
| `parameters.max-connections` | int (PositiveInt) | _unset_ | Allows for setting the maximum number of db connections used by Canton and the ledger API server. If None, the value will be auto-detected from the number of processors. Has no effect, if the number of connections is already set via slick options (i.e., `config.numThreads`). |
| `parameters.migrate-and-start` | boolean | `false` | if true, db migrations will be applied to the database (default is to abort start if db migrates are pending to force an explicit upgrade) |
| `parameters.migrations-paths` | array of string | `[]` | Where should database migrations be read from. Enables specialized DDL for different database servers (e.g. Postgres). |
| `parameters.partitions.initial-bft-orderer-tables-partition-size` | int | `1500` | Initial partition size for bft-orderer tables. Note that this config is only read once, during the initial database setup and later changes to this value won't have any effect. This is also only used in Postgres setups. |
| `parameters.repeatable-migrations-paths` | array of string | `[]` | Additional locations to scan for migrations. The goal is to be able to provide additional database table settings under this path, i.e. vacuum/analyze settings for Postgres. The paths should only contain repeatable migrations (files like `R__table_settings.sql`). |
| `parameters.unsafe-baseline-on-migrate` | boolean | `false` | TO BE USED ONLY FOR TESTING! &lt;p&gt;Whether to automatically call baseline when migrate is executed against a non-empty schema with no schema history table. This schema will then be baselined with the \{@code baselineVersion\} before executing the migrations. Only migrations above \{@code baselineVersion\} will then be applied.&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;This is useful for databases projects where the initial vendor schema is not empty&lt;/p&gt; If baseline should be called on migrate for non-empty schemas, \{ @code false\} if not. (default: \{ @code false\}) |
| `parameters.unsafe-clean-on-validation-error` | boolean | `false` | TO BE USED ONLY FOR TESTING! Clean the database if validation during DB migration fails. |
| `parameters.warn-on-slow-query` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | _unset_ | Optional time when we start logging a query as slow. |
| `parameters.warn-on-slow-query-interval` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) | `5 seconds` | How often to repeat the logging statement for slow queries. |

</Tab>
</Tabs>
